tasks

Tasks, upcoming tasks, task bulk status.

GET/tasks

List tasks

One page of tasks. Roles: every authenticated role. Admins see every task and may use search and sorting. Every other role is silently narrowed to the tasks assigned to them, and search, sort_by and sort_order are ignored for them, except with label_id, which is the one non-admin path that honours sort_by and sort_order. label_id filters to the tasks carrying that label and takes precedence over search (search is ignored when both are sent); an unparseable or non-positive label_id is ignored, and an unknown one yields an empty page rather than 404. Precedence for admins: label_id, then search, then sort_by, then the plain list. search matches title or description with SQL LIKE. An unknown sort_by is ignored (unsorted, ascending id) and any sort_order other than asc or desc falls back to asc. Pagination: page (1-based) overrides offset when greater than 0; offset defaults to 0; limit defaults to 20, non-positive or unparseable values fall back to the default and anything above 100 is clamped to 100. The non-admin path without label_id returns assigned_to as a zero-valued object. lead_id and lead are omitted whenever the caller may not see the linked lead (admins see every lead, sales users only the leads they own, every other role none), decided again on every read.

Authentication: BearerAuth (Authorization header), ApiKeyAuth (Authorization header) · Response: application/json

ParameterInTypeDescription
pagequeryinteger

Page number (1-based); overrides offset when greater than 0

offsetqueryinteger

Number of tasks to skip

default 0
limitqueryinteger

Page size; clamped to 100

default 20
sort_byquerystring

Sort column (admin, or any role with label_id); unknown values are ignored

one of: "created_at", "updated_at", "title", "status", "priority", "due_date"
sort_orderquerystring

Sort direction; anything else falls back to asc

POST/tasks

Create a new task

Create a task. Roles: admin, sales and support (customers get 403 before the body is read). Non-admin callers may only assign the task to themselves; admins may assign it to any active user. The task always starts in status pending, whatever the body says, and priority defaults to medium. A task may link a lead or a customer, but not both. lead_id must name a lead the caller may see (admins any lead, sales users only the leads they own, every other role none); a hidden lead is answered with 404 exactly like a missing one. label_ids attaches existing labels (a repeated id is attached once); any id that matches no label rejects the whole request. The checks run in this order: role, body binding, self-assignment, assignee (exists, active), lead, customer, lead-and-customer conflict, labels. No automation event and no notification is produced. lead_id and lead are omitted whenever the caller may not see the linked lead (admins see every lead, sales users only the leads they own, every other role none), decided again on every read.

No automation event is recorded; once the id, status and role checks have passed, an audit row is written to the bulk operation log and marked completed or failed.

GET/tasks/upcoming

List tasks due in the next N days

The open tasks whose due date falls between now and N days from now, as a bare array ordered by due date ascending (then id). Roles: every authenticated role; admins see every assignee's tasks, every other role only the tasks assigned to them. Completed and cancelled tasks and tasks without a due date are excluded; the window starts at the current instant, so overdue tasks are NOT included. An absent, unparseable or non-positive days value falls back to 7, and anything above 90 is clamped to 90 rather than rejected. At most 100 tasks are returned and there is no pagination. Labels are not loaded here (labels is omitted). lead_id and lead are omitted whenever the caller may not see the linked lead (admins see every lead, sales users only the leads they own, every other role none), decided again on every read.

PUT/tasks/{id}

Update a task

Partially update a task: absent or empty fields are left unchanged (title, description, due_date and the links cannot be cleared this way). Roles: every authenticated role; admins may update any task, every other role only tasks assigned to them. Only admins may change assigned_to_id; sending the current assignee back is not a reassignment. A completed task cannot be moved to another status. A task cannot link both a lead and a customer. A lead_id that is sent must name a lead the caller may see (a hidden lead is 404 like a missing one), even when it equals the stored link, so sending lead ids cannot reveal a hidden link; a stored link is kept unchecked when both link fields are absent. Sending customer_id without lead_id replaces the association: the lead link is cleared and the customer link set after validation, whether or not the caller could see the old lead. label_ids, when present, replaces the whole label set (an empty array clears it); absent leaves the labels untouched; any id that matches no label rejects the whole request. The order of checks: id, body binding, task lookup, ownership, reassignment permission, then assignee, lead, customer, conflict, completed status, labels; any failure leaves the task unchanged. No automation event is produced. lead_id and lead are omitted whenever the caller may not see the linked lead (admins see every lead, sales users only the leads they own, every other role none), decided again on every read.

POST/automation/rules

Create an automation rule

Create a follow-up automation rule with its ordered steps. Roles: admin only. entity_type is lead or deal; trigger_type is lead_created, lead_updated, lead_status_changed, deal_created, deal_updated, deal_stage_changed or schedule; trigger carries from/to for a status or stage change and hour_utc, filters and stale_days for a schedule. Steps are wait, condition, create_task, assign_owner, send_email and notify, numbered 1..n in the given order. Validation (nothing is written when it fails): name required, at most 200 characters; description at most 65535 bytes; an invalid trigger or step config, more than 20 steps, more than 90 days of waits, a backward or out-of-range else_position or an unknown placeholder is 400 INVALID_INPUT; an unknown, inactive or customer user id is 400 INVALID_REFERENCE when it is the first refusal. Every refused field is listed in error.details.fields as {field, code, message}: field is a path into the body (name, trigger.hour_utc, steps[i].config.title with i counted from zero), code one of required, too_long, line_break, invalid_value, out_of_range, not_applicable, unknown_placeholder, too_many, unknown_reference; error.message and error.code are those of the first refused field. An unknown entity_type stops the checks, an unknown trigger_type skips the trigger checks, and no steps or more than 20 skips the step checks. Defaults are stored: recipient owner, else stop. A new rule is version 1, records the caller as created_by_id, and is disabled unless enabled is true. Runs are started by the background worker, which matches each event against the rules that are enabled when it dispatches the event.

Authentication: BearerAuth (Authorization header), ApiKeyAuth (Authorization header) · Request: application/json · Response: application/json

ParameterInTypeDescription
requestrequiredbodyhandler.AutomationRuleRequest

The rule

StatusBodyDescription
201utils.APIResponse with data: models.AutomationRule

Rule created with its steps

400utils.APIResponse with error: utils.APIError & object {details: handler.AutomationRuleErrorDetails}

INVALID_INPUT - the rule body was refused (error.details.fields lists every refused field; error.message and error.code are those of the first); INVALID_REFERENCE - the same, when the first refusal is an unknown, deactivated or customer user_id; BAD_REQUEST (message Invalid request format) when the body is not valid JSON or a value has the wrong JSON type

401utils.APIResponse with error: utils.APIError

UNAUTHORIZED - missing or malformed Authorization header, or invalid, expired or revoked credentials

403utils.APIResponse with error: utils.APIError

FORBIDDEN - admin role required (route guard)

Simulate an unsaved rule body against one live record: the rule editor's current values, for a new rule or for unsaved edits of an existing one. Roles: admin only. The body goes through the same normalisation and validation as the create (400 INVALID_INPUT or INVALID_REFERENCE with error.details.fields), then is simulated like POST /automation/rules/{id}/dry-run. Nothing is saved, no transaction is started and nothing is written, sent or notified; a saved rule's version and steps are untouched. PUT/automation/rules/{id}

Update an automation rule

Replace the rule wholesale with the same body as the create. Roles: admin only. The body is validated first (the same 400 answers and error.details.fields as the create, before the rule is looked up), then in one transaction the rule row is locked, the version incremented, the steps replaced (new step ids) and the rule's pending, waiting and running runs cancelled with cancel_reason rule_changed. enabled absent keeps the stored switch as read under the row lock, so a disable committed meanwhile is not undone. created_by_id is kept. Any failure leaves the rule, its steps and its runs untouched.

Authentication: BearerAuth (Authorization header), ApiKeyAuth (Authorization header) · Request: application/json · Response: application/json

ParameterInTypeDescription
idrequiredpathinteger

Rule ID

requestrequiredbodyhandler.AutomationRuleRequest

POST/automation/rules/{id}/dry-run

Dry-run an automation rule

Simulate a saved rule against one live record of its entity type and report each step's would-be outcome: the outcome, the simulated time, the resolved user ids, the task due date and link, the owner from/to and the branch taken. Roles: admin only. Waits advance the simulated clock without pausing, an earlier simulated assign feeds later conditions and recipients, and steps skipped by a condition branch are not listed. The rule is simulated whether or not it is enabled. Nothing is written, sent or notified. Order of checks: id, body binding, rule lookup, entity type match, record lookup.

GET/automation/runs

List automation runs

One page of runs, without their step logs. Roles: admin sees every run; sales only runs whose lead or deal it currently owns (applied inside the query, so meta.total counts that set; ownership is read at request time, so it follows reassignments); support and customers get 403 from the route guard. Filters: status, rule_id, entity_type, entity_id (each validated, 400 when malformed). sort_by is limited to the listed columns (anything else is 400). An absent sort_by sorts by created_at descending and then ignores sort_order; with a sort_by, sort_order is asc or desc and anything else means desc. Ties are broken by id in the same direction. Pagination: page (1-based) overrides offset when greater than 0; limit defaults to 20 and is clamped to 100; the checks run before any query, sort first. Each run carries rule_name, the rule's current name (also for a deleted rule, then with rule_deleted true), and never the rule's trigger or steps; a sales user therefore sees the names of the rules that ran on its records.

Authentication: BearerAuth (Authorization header), ApiKeyAuth (Authorization header) · Response: application/json

aeo

Answer-engine visibility tracking: profile, prompts, runs, reports.

GET/aeo/citations

AEO citation report

Which sources the non-error answers cite over the requested window: the owned-domain citation rate, per-company citation and brand-mention rates (the brand first, then each competitor in profile order, companies without citations included) and the twenty most cited hosts by citation count descending. Windows are 7, 30 or 90 days; anything else falls back to 30. Works before a brand profile exists (by_company is then empty).

Roles: admin, sales, support.

GET/aeo/dashboard

AEO visibility dashboard

Aggregated visibility over the requested window: the overall percentage of non-error answers that mention the brand, the same figure per engine, a daily timeline with one entry per UTC day (days without answers carry overall 0 and no per-engine entries), the share of voice across the brand and its competitors, and the per-company daily timeline. Windows are 7, 30 or 90 days; anything else falls back to 30. The window ends (exclusive) at the start of tomorrow in UTC, so today's answers are included. Works before a brand profile exists: the brand figures are then empty and share of voice falls back to the competitor names found in the answers.

Roles: admin, sales, support.

PUT/aeo/profile

Create or update the AEO brand profile

Save the single brand profile; the first call creates it, later calls replace it wholesale (a list left out of the body is cleared). The service trims every value, drops blank aliases, domains and competitors, de-duplicates aliases and domains case-insensitively, and reduces domains to bare lower-case hosts (scheme, path and a leading "www." removed). The response is the stored row. Only answers collected after the save are analysed against the new profile; stored answers keep their mention and citation analysis.

Roles: admin, sales.

Errors: 400 VALIDATION_ERROR (message "Validation failed", error.details maps the failing Go struct field name, e.g. BrandName or Prompts[0], to a message) when a binding rule fails; 400 BAD_REQUEST ("Invalid request format") for a body that is not valid JSON or does not match the field types; 400 VALIDATION_ERROR without error.details (the member is omitted) when the brand name is blank once trimmed or a value is too long after trimming (message names the rule, e.g. "brand name is required: invalid brand profile").

Authentication: BearerAuth (Authorization header), ApiKeyAuth (Authorization header) · Request: application/json · Response: application/json

Complete brand profile

GET/aeo/prompts

List tracked AEO prompts

One page of tracked prompts (soft-deleted prompts are never listed). Each carries computed figures over the requested window: visibility (share of non-error answers that mention the brand, percentage 0..100 with one decimal), the answer and mention counts behind it and the time of the most recent answer. The window is the 7/30/90-day selector the dashboard uses; any other or unparseable value falls back to 30.

Pagination: offset (default 0) and limit (default 20, capped at 100; non-positive or unparseable values fall back to the default). meta carries page, per_page, total (prompts matching active_only) and total_pages.

Sorting: sort_by is one of id, text, is_active, created_at, updated_at; any other value is answered with 400 BAD_REQUEST ("Invalid sort column: <value>"). sort_order is asc or desc; anything else means desc. Without sort_by the order is created_at descending whatever sort_order says. Ties are broken by id in the same direction.

Roles: admin, sales, support.

POST/aeo/prompts

Add tracked AEO prompts

Add 1 to 25 prompts in one request; new prompts are always active. Texts are trimmed and must be 1 to 500 characters. The batch is all-or-nothing and runs in one transaction: a repeat within the request or a text matching a live prompt (case-insensitive; soft-deleted prompts do not reserve their text) saves nothing and answers 409 CONFLICT, and so does nothing when the batch would push the number of active prompts above 100 (400). The response lists the created prompts in request order; their computed visibility figures are 0.

Roles: admin, sales.

Errors: 400 VALIDATION_ERROR (message "Validation failed", error.details maps the failing Go struct field name, e.g. BrandName or Prompts[0], to a message) when a binding rule fails; 400 BAD_REQUEST ("Invalid request format") for a body that is not valid JSON or does not match the field types; 400 VALIDATION_ERROR without error.details (the member is omitted) when an entry is blank once trimmed ("prompt text must be between 1 and 500 characters") or the batch would exceed 100 active prompts ("active prompt limit of 100 reached"); 409 CONFLICT naming the duplicated text.

Authentication: BearerAuth (Authorization header), ApiKeyAuth (Authorization header) · Request: application/json · Response: application/json

ParameterInTypeDescription
requestrequiredbodyhandler.CreateAEOPromptsRequest

Prompts to add

POST/aeo/prompts/generate

Generate candidate AEO prompts

Ask the generation engine for buyer-style questions derived from the brand profile. The engine is the integration.aeo.generation_engine setting (default anthropic; openai, gemini, kimi or perplexity are the other choices) and generation never falls back to another engine. Its API key is read from the admin settings first and from the engine's environment variable otherwise (ANTHROPIC_API_KEY, OPENAI_API_KEY, GEMINI_API_KEY, MOONSHOT_API_KEY, PERPLEXITY_API_KEY). The call is bounded by a 90-second timeout. Nothing is stored: the suggestions come back as plain strings (de-duplicated, each 12 to 500 characters, at most count of them) and are only tracked once POSTed to /aeo/prompts. The body is optional; an empty body asks for 10.

Side effects: one billed request to the external engine.

Roles: admin, sales.

Errors: 400 VALIDATION_ERROR (message "Validation failed", error.details maps the failing Go struct field name, e.g. BrandName or Prompts[0], to a message) when a binding rule fails; 400 BAD_REQUEST ("Invalid request format") for a body that is not valid JSON or does not match the field types; 409 CONFLICT when the brand profile has not been configured; 503 PROVIDER_NOT_CONFIGURED when the selected engine has no API key (the message names the engine); 503 PROVIDER_REJECTED when the engine answered with an HTTP error ("the generation engine rejected the request (HTTP 401) — check its API key in the AEO settings" for a 4xx, "the generation engine is unavailable (HTTP 529); try again later" otherwise). 503 rather than 502 so the JSON body survives proxies such as Cloudflare. A failure without an HTTP status (timeout, DNS, TLS, egress policy) and an answer with no usable suggestion are 500 INTERNAL_ERROR.

Authentication: BearerAuth (Authorization header), ApiKeyAuth (Authorization header) · Request: application/json · Response: application/json

ParameterInTypeDescription
requestbodyhandler.GenerateAEOPromptsRequest

How many suggestions to generate (1..25; absent or 0 means 10)

StatusBodyDescription
200utils.APIResponse with data: handler.GeneratedAEOPromptsData

Suggestions generated successfully (data.prompts)

400utils.APIResponse with error: utils.APIError

VALIDATION_ERROR or BAD_REQUEST - count negative or above 25 (0 means the default 10), or a malformed body

401utils.APIResponse with error: utils.APIError

UNAUTHORIZED - missing or malformed Authorization header, or an invalid or expired token or API key

403utils.APIResponse with error: utils.APIError

FORBIDDEN - role is not admin or sales

409utils.APIResponse with error: utils.APIError

CONFLICT - the brand profile has not been configured yet

429utils.APIResponse with error: utils.APIError

TOO_MANY_REQUESTS - moderate tier: 120 requests/min, burst 30, per client IP, shared by every authenticated route; error.details.retry_after is '60s'; no Retry-After header

500utils.APIResponse with error: utils.APIError

PROVIDER_NOT_CONFIGURED - the selected generation engine has no API key; PROVIDER_REJECTED - the engine answered with an HTTP error

PUT/aeo/prompts/{id}

Update a tracked AEO prompt

Edit a prompt's text and/or toggle whether full runs include it. Both fields are optional and only the ones present are applied, so deactivating never rewrites the text; a body of {} changes nothing but still answers 200. Stored answers keep pointing at the prompt whatever the edit. The response carries the prompt without computed figures (visibility and counts are 0, last_run_at is omitted).

Roles: admin, sales.

Errors: 400 BAD_REQUEST for a path id that is not an unsigned integer ("Invalid prompt ID") or a text that is blank once trimmed ("Prompt text cannot be blank"); 400 VALIDATION_ERROR (message "Validation failed", error.details maps the failing Go struct field name, e.g. BrandName or Prompts[0], to a message) when a binding rule fails; 400 BAD_REQUEST ("Invalid request format") for a body that is not valid JSON or does not match the field types (an empty body is BAD_REQUEST); 400 VALIDATION_ERROR when reactivating a prompt while 100 are already active; 404 NOT_FOUND for an unknown or deleted prompt; 409 CONFLICT when another live prompt has the same text (case-insensitive).

POST/aeo/prompts/{id}/run

Run a single AEO prompt

Queue ONE prompt against every configured engine and return at once with the run row (trigger "manual", status "running"). The prompt may be inactive, which is how a draft is tested before it joins full runs; a soft-deleted prompt is 404. The same single-run-in-flight guard applies as for a full run.

Side effects: one billed request per configured engine; one answer row per call is stored.

Roles: admin, sales.

Errors: 400 VALIDATION_ERROR for an id that is not a positive integer (message "Validation failed", error.details is the string "Invalid prompt id"); Checks run in this order and the first failing one answers: 503 SHUTTING_DOWN while the server is draining for shutdown; 409 CONFLICT when the brand profile has not been configured; 503 PROVIDERS_UNAVAILABLE when no engine is configured; 409 CONFLICT when a run is already in progress (runs left "running" for more than 6 hours are first marked failed, so they do not block); 404 NOT_FOUND for an unknown prompt ("Prompt not found").

GET/aeo/providers

List the AEO answer engines

Every supported engine with the model it would use and whether it is configured, re-read on every call so a key just stored in the settings shows up at once: anthropic, openai, gemini, kimi, perplexity, then the custom engine under its configured name (AEO_CUSTOM_NAME, "custom" when unset). A named engine counts as configured when it has an API key, the custom engine when it has a base URL. Unconfigured engines are skipped by runs. API keys are never returned. Always six entries, never paginated.

POST/aeo/runs

Start an AEO run

Queue every active prompt against every configured engine (trigger "manual") and return at once with the run row in status "running"; total_queries is prompts x engines. The work continues in the background and outlives the request, so poll GET /aeo/runs/{id} for progress. Only one run may be in flight at a time, manual and scheduled runs alike.

Side effects: one billed request per prompt and engine to the external engines; one answer row (with its citations) is stored per call, failed calls included.

Roles: admin, sales.

Errors: Checks run in this order and the first failing one answers: 503 SHUTTING_DOWN while the server is draining for shutdown; 409 CONFLICT when the brand profile has not been configured; 503 PROVIDERS_UNAVAILABLE when no engine is configured; 409 CONFLICT when a run is already in progress (runs left "running" for more than 6 hours are first marked failed, so they do not block); 404 NOT_FOUND when there is no active prompt ("No active AEO prompts to run").

POST/forms

Create a form

Create a form definition. The server assigns the public identifier used by the embed script and records the caller as author. The definition is normalised and validated before it is stored: name trimmed and non-blank; 1 to 50 fields with unique machine names matching ^[a-z][a-z0-9_]{0,49}$; exactly one field of type email, named "email"; select fields with 1 to 50 non-blank options and no options on other types; hidden fields never required; max_length defaulted and clamped (see FormFieldDef); a redirect action needs an http(s) redirect_url; notification addresses shaped like email addresses; allowed domains bare hosts. Omitting create_lead means true, and then default_owner_id must name an active admin or sales user. status defaults to draft, submit_action to message. The response is the stored form.

Roles: admin, sales.

Errors: 400 VALIDATION_ERROR (message "Validation failed", error.details maps the failing Go struct field name, e.g. Name or Fields, to a message) when a binding rule fails; 400 BAD_REQUEST ("Invalid request format") for a body that is not valid JSON or does not match the field types; 400 VALIDATION_ERROR without error.details (the member is omitted) when a definition rule fails (the message states the rule, e.g. "a form that creates leads needs a default owner: invalid form definition: validation failed"); 400 VALIDATION_ERROR (message "Validation failed") with error.details {"default_owner_id": "..."} when the lead owner does not exist, is deactivated or is not an admin or sales user.

Authentication: BearerAuth (Authorization header), ApiKeyAuth (Authorization header) · Request: application/json · Response: application/json

200utils.APIResponse with data: array of models.FormSubmission, meta: utils.APIMeta

Submissions retrieved successfully

400utils.APIResponse with error: utils.APIError

BAD_REQUEST - the id is not a positive integer ('Invalid form ID')

401utils.APIResponse with error: utils.APIError

UNAUTHORIZED - missing or malformed Authorization header, or an invalid or expired token or API key

403utils.APIResponse with error: utils.APIError

FORBIDDEN - role is not admin, sales or support (the customer role is always refused)

404utils.APIResponse with error: utils.APIError

NOT_FOUND - form not found or deleted

429utils.APIResponse with error: utils.APIError

TOO_MANY_REQUESTS - moderate tier: 120 requests/min, burst 30, per client IP, shared by every authenticated route; error.details.retry_after is '60s'; no Retry-After header

500utils.APIResponse with error: utils.APIError

INTERNAL_ERROR - unexpected failure; the cause is never echoed

public-forms

Unauthenticated endpoints reached by visitors of the sites a form is embedded on: definition, submission, embed script, view page and double opt-in confirmation. Some return JavaScript or HTML instead of the JSON envelope; CORS is open to any origin without credentials.

GET/forms/public/confirm

Double opt-in confirmation page

The page the link in a double opt-in confirmation mail opens (PUBLIC_BASE_URL + API prefix + /forms/public/confirm?token=...). It renders an HTML page with a "Confirm email" button that POSTs the token to /forms/public/confirm. A GET never spends or even checks the token, so mail scanners and link previews cannot confirm on the visitor's behalf; any non-empty token gets the button page, a missing or blank one gets the generic "This link is no longer valid" page. The handler always answers 200 text/html, never the JSON envelope; only the rate limiter in front of it answers in JSON (429 TOO_MANY_REQUESTS).

Unauthenticated.

Authentication: none (public) · Response: text/html, application/json

ParameterInTypeDescription
tokenquerystring

Confirmation token from the mailed link

StatusBodyDescription
200string

HTML page: the confirmation button, or the invalid-link page when the token is missing (text/html; charset=utf-8)

429utils.APIResponse with error: utils.APIError

TOO_MANY_REQUESTS (JSON envelope) - strict tier: 10 requests/min, burst 5, per client IP, one bucket shared by public submissions and both confirmation routes (lifted by DISABLE_RATE_LIMIT=true); error.details.retry_after is '60s'; no Retry-After header

POST/forms/public/confirm

Confirm a double opt-in submission

Spend a confirmation token. The token is read from the form-encoded field token (what the confirmation page posts) or, when that is empty, from a JSON body {"token": "..."}. On success the pending submission becomes confirmed (confirmed_at set) and, in the same transaction, the token is claimed and the lead is created or noted exactly as for a submission without double opt-in (automation event lead_created or lead_updated, origin form); after the commit the notification mails and the follow-up mail go out, and the response is the "Email confirmed" page. Tokens are single-use and expire 48 hours after the submission; a re-submission of the same form and address invalidates earlier tokens.

Every failure - missing, unknown, expired or already used token, a submission that is no longer pending, a deleted form or submission, a stored value too long for its lead column, or an internal error - renders the same "This link is no longer valid" page with the same 200 status, so token validity cannot be probed. A failure after the claim and before the commit rolls back, so the link stays usable for a retry; when the COMMIT itself fails the outcome is ambiguous (the lead may have been created and the link spent) although the page still says the link is no longer valid. The handler always answers 200 text/html, never the JSON envelope; only the rate limiter in front of it answers in JSON (429 TOO_MANY_REQUESTS).

Unauthenticated.

Authentication: none (public) · Request: application/x-www-form-urlencoded, application/json · Response: text/html, application/json

ParameterInTypeDescription
tokenformDatastring

Confirmation token (form-encoded; a JSON body with a token field is accepted too)

StatusBodyDescription
200string

HTML page: 'Email confirmed' on success, otherwise the generic invalid-link page (text/html; charset=utf-8)

429utils.APIResponse with error: utils.APIError

TOO_MANY_REQUESTS (JSON envelope) - strict tier: 10 requests/min, burst 5, per client IP, one bucket shared by public submissions and both confirmation routes (lifted by DISABLE_RATE_LIMIT=true); error.details.retry_after is '60s'; no Retry-After header

GET/forms/public/embed.js

Form embed script

The JavaScript renderer that external pages load with <script src=".../forms/public/embed.js" data-form-key="<public_id>" async></script>. It fetches the definition from GET /forms/public/{key}, renders the form and posts submissions to POST /forms/public/{key}/submissions. Compiled into the server binary, so it always matches the API version. Served as raw JavaScript, never in the JSON envelope, with Cache-Control "public, max-age=3600"; only the rate limiter in front of it answers in JSON (429 TOO_MANY_REQUESTS).

Unauthenticated: no credentials are needed or accepted. Cross-origin calls get a permissive, credential-less CORS policy (the request Origin is echoed, methods GET, POST, OPTIONS, header Content-Type; preflights answer 204).

Authentication: none (public) · Response: application/javascript, application/json

StatusBodyDescription
200string

The renderer script (application/javascript; charset=utf-8)

429utils.APIResponse with error: utils.APIError

TOO_MANY_REQUESTS (JSON envelope) - generous tier: 240 requests/min, burst 40, per client IP, one bucket shared by GET embed.js, GET {key} and GET {key}/view; error.details.retry_after is '60s'; no Retry-After header

GET/forms/public/{key}

Get a public form definition

Everything a visitor's browser needs to render a published form: its name and public id, the ordered field definitions (with the max_length the server enforces), the consent text, the submit action, the reCAPTCHA site key when the form uses reCAPTCHA and the server has both keys, the name of the honeypot input and a freshly signed time-trap challenge that the submission must carry back. Staff-only settings (notification addresses, mail texts, lead owner, allowlist) are never included.

The origin is taken from the Origin header, or from Referer when Origin is absent. When the form has an allowlist, a request whose origin host is not on it, or that carries neither header, is refused. Unknown, unpublished (draft, archived), deleted and origin-restricted forms are all the same 404 NOT_FOUND (message "Form not found"); the response never says which.

Unauthenticated: no credentials are needed or accepted. Cross-origin calls get a permissive, credential-less CORS policy (the request Origin is echoed, methods GET, POST, OPTIONS, header Content-Type; preflights answer 204).

Authentication: none (public) · Response: application/json

ParameterInTypeDescription
keyrequiredpathstring

Public form identifier (the form's public_id)

StatusBodyDescription
200utils.APIResponse with data: service.PublicFormDefinition

Form definition retrieved successfully

404utils.APIResponse with error: utils.APIError

NOT_FOUND - no published form with this identifier is available to this origin

429utils.APIResponse with error: utils.APIError

TOO_MANY_REQUESTS (JSON envelope) - generous tier: 240 requests/min, burst 40, per client IP, one bucket shared by GET embed.js, GET {key} and GET {key}/view; error.details.retry_after is '60s'; no Retry-After header

500utils.APIResponse with error: utils.APIError

INTERNAL_ERROR - unexpected failure

POST/forms/public/{key}/submissions

Submit a public form

Accept a submission for a published form. The body carries the field values, the consent flag, the challenge handed out with the definition, the honeypot input, the reCAPTCHA token when the form uses it, and the page URL. Bodies are capped at 64 KiB (65536 bytes).

Checks run in a fixed order and the first that trips decides: (1) values are validated against the definition; (2) the challenge must be present and correctly signed; then the spam layers: (3) time trap - submitted less than 3 seconds or more than 24 hours after the definition was served; (4) origin - the Origin header (or Referer) host must be on the form's allowlist when it has one; (5) honeypot - the decoy input must be empty; (6) reCAPTCHA v3, only when the form enables it and the server has both keys - a missing token, a score below RECAPTCHA_MIN_SCORE or an unreachable verification service fails it.

A submission caught by a spam layer is stored with status spam and its reason and answered with exactly the same 200 outcome a genuine one gets, so the response reveals nothing about the layers; no mail is sent and no lead is touched. This is deliberate.

Side effects of a genuine submission: without double opt-in it is stored as received; when the form creates leads, the newest live lead with the same email gains a note (automation event lead_updated, origin form) or a new lead is created (status new, owner default_owner_id, source the form name; automation event lead_created, origin form), in the same transaction; after the commit one notification mail goes to each notify_emails address and, when follow_up_subject is set, the follow-up mail goes to the submitter. With double opt-in it is stored as pending, earlier outstanding confirmation links for the same form and address are invalidated, and a confirmation mail with a single-use link valid for 48 hours goes to the submitted address; lead, notification and follow-up wait for POST /forms/public/confirm. Mail failures are logged and never change the response.

Errors: 400 VALIDATION_ERROR (message "The submission could not be accepted") with error.details mapping field names to messages - an undeclared field ("unknown field"), a missing required value, a value longer than its max_length, an invalid email, a value that is not one of a select's options, "consent" when the form has consent text and consent is not true, or "challenge" when the challenge is missing or invalid (reported only once the values are valid); 400 BAD_REQUEST ("Invalid request body") for a body that is not valid JSON; 404 NOT_FOUND for an unknown, unpublished or deleted form (an origin outside the allowlist is NOT a 404 here: it is stored as spam); 413 with code VALIDATION_ERROR ("Submission is too large") for a body over 64 KiB.

Unauthenticated: no credentials are needed or accepted. Cross-origin calls get a permissive, credential-less CORS policy (the request Origin is echoed, methods GET, POST, OPTIONS, header Content-Type; preflights answer 204).

Authentication: none (public) · Request: application/json · Response: application/json

ParameterInTypeDescription
keyrequiredpathstring

Public form identifier (the form's public_id)

submissionrequiredbodyservice.PublicSubmissionRequest

Submitted values

StatusBodyDescription
200utils.APIResponse with data: service.SubmitOutcome

Submission accepted (or silently filed as spam); the outcome says whether to show a message or redirect

400utils.APIResponse with error: utils.APIError

VALIDATION_ERROR - error.details maps field names to messages; BAD_REQUEST - malformed JSON

404utils.APIResponse with error: utils.APIError

NOT_FOUND - no published form with this identifier

413utils.APIResponse with error: utils.APIError

VALIDATION_ERROR - submission body exceeds 64 KiB

429utils.APIResponse with error: utils.APIError

TOO_MANY_REQUESTS (JSON envelope) - strict tier: 10 requests/min, burst 5, per client IP, one bucket shared by public submissions and both confirmation routes (lifted by DISABLE_RATE_LIMIT=true); error.details.retry_after is '60s'; no Retry-After header

500utils.APIResponse with error: utils.APIError

INTERNAL_ERROR - unexpected failure, including a request cancelled during the reCAPTCHA check

GET/forms/public/{key}/view

Hosted form page

A standalone HTML page (shareable URL) that loads the embed script for this key, so a form can be used without embedding it anywhere. The key is not checked: the page is the same 200 text/html shell for any key, and only the script's own definition request decides whether a form appears (an unknown, unpublished or origin-restricted key renders nothing). The definition request from this page normally carries no Origin header but a Referer naming this server, so a form with an allowlist renders here only when this server's host is listed. The page is marked noindex, nofollow and needs JavaScript.

Unauthenticated.

Authentication: none (public) · Response: text/html, application/json

ParameterInTypeDescription
keyrequiredpathstring

Public form identifier (the form's public_id)

StatusBodyDescription
200string

HTML page (text/html; charset=utf-8)

429utils.APIResponse with error: utils.APIError

TOO_MANY_REQUESTS (JSON envelope) - generous tier: 240 requests/min, burst 40, per client IP, one bucket shared by GET embed.js, GET {key} and GET {key}/view; error.details.retry_after is '60s'; no Retry-After header

Opaque single-use refresh token (43 URL-safe base64 characters) for POST /auth/refresh, valid for REFRESH_TOKEN_EXPIRY_DAYS (30 days by default). Absent on registration, and on a login whose refresh token could not be stored (the login then succeeds with the access token only).

handler.CreateAEOPromptsRequest

FieldTypeDescription
promptsrequiredarray of string

Prompt texts to track: 1 to 25 entries, each non-empty and at most 500 characters. Entries are trimmed; a whitespace-only entry, a repeat within the request (case-insensitive) or a text matching a live prompt rejects the whole batch.

min items 1; max items 25; example ["What is the best self-hosted CRM for a small sales team?", "Which CRM tools support embeddable lead-capture forms?"]

handler.FormListItem

FieldTypeDescription
allowed_domainsarray of string

Origin allowlist: bare host names with an optional port, lower-cased, matched exactly (subdomains must be listed one by one). Empty allows every origin. A definition requested from another origin is a 404; a submission from another origin is stored as spam (reason domain). At most 20, each at most 255 characters. Reads return [] when there are none; the POST /forms and PUT /forms/{id} responses echo the request and carry null when the list was omitted or sent as null.

nullable; example ["www.example.com", "localhost:5173"]

max length 255; example "Please confirm your email address"

max length 512; example "https://www.example.com/files/guide.pdf"

Ordered field definitions (1 to 50), normalised on save (names trimmed, max_length defaulted and clamped).

max length 200; example "Acme rollout - phase 1 (revised)"

models.AutomationStep

One ordered step of an automation rule.

FieldTypeDescription
configmodels.AutomationStepConfig

The step configuration as stored, with defaults filled in (recipient owner, else stop); which fields apply depends on step_type.

created_atstring

When the step row was written (RFC 3339). Read-only.

read-only; example "2026-10-30T08:00:00Z"

Unique identifier of the step row. Read-only; a PUT of the rule replaces the steps with new ids.

read-only; example 301

1-based position of the step in the rule, the order of the steps array in the request. Read-only.

read-only; example 1

ID of the rule the step belongs to. Read-only.

What the step does: wait pauses the run, condition tests the record and stops or jumps when false, create_task creates a task, assign_owner changes the record's owner, send_email emails a staff user, notify creates an in-app notification.

one of: "wait", "condition", "create_task", "assign_owner", "send_email", "notify"; example "create_task"

models.AutomationStepConfig

Configuration of one automation step. Which fields apply depends on step_type: wait (duration_minutes), condition (field, operator, value, currency, else, else_position), create_task (title, description, priority, due_in_days, recipient, user_id), assign_owner (user_id), send_email (subject, body, recipient, user_id), notify (title, body, recipient, user_id).

FieldTypeDescription
bodystring

send_email (required) and notify (optional): the body template, at most 65535 bytes, with the notify placeholders. An email is sent as plain text to the recipient's address.

example "Please contact {{ lead.first_name }} {{ lead.last_name }} today."
currencystring

condition on amount_cents only: the ISO currency (three upper-case letters) the amount is in. Required for amount_cents and refused on any other field; a deal in another currency makes the condition false, amounts in different currencies are never compared.

example "EUR"
descriptionstring

create_task only: the task description template, at most 65535 bytes, with the task placeholders of title.

example "Lead status is {{ lead.status }}."
due_in_daysinteger

create_task only: the new task is due this many days after the step runs, 0 to 365.

minimum 0; maximum 365; example 2
duration_minutesinteger

wait: how long the run pauses, in minutes, 1 to 129600 (90 days). The waits of one rule may add up to at most 90 days.

minimum 1; maximum 129600; example 1440
elsestring

condition: what happens when the condition is false. stop ends the run; jump continues at else_position. Empty is stored as stop.

one of: "stop", "jump"; example "jump"
else_positioninteger

condition with else jump only: the 1-based position to continue at, which must come after the condition step and be at most the step count. Refused (not_applicable) with else stop.

minimum 2; maximum 20; example 4
fieldstring

condition: the record field compared. Lead rules: status, source, classification, company (compared as strings) and owner_id (a number). Deal rules: stage, source, currency (strings), amount_cents (money, needs currency) and probability, owner_id (numbers). Names, addresses and notes are never comparable.

one of: "status", "source", "classification", "company", "owner_id", "stage", "currency", "amount_cents", "probability"; example "status"
operatorstring

condition: the comparison. String fields take eq, neq, in, not_in, contains (case-insensitive substring), is_empty and is_not_empty; number and money fields take eq, neq, gt, gte, lt and lte.

one of: "eq", "neq", "in", "not_in", "contains", "is_empty", "is_not_empty", "gt", "gte", "lt", "lte"; example "eq"
prioritystring

create_task only: the new task's priority; empty means medium.

one of: "low", "medium", "high"; example "high"
recipientstring

create_task, send_email, notify: who receives the task, the email or the notification. owner is the record's owner at execution time; user is the staff user named by user_id. Empty is stored as owner. assign_owner always stores user.

one of: "owner", "user"; example "owner"
subjectstring

send_email only (required): the subject template, at most 255 characters and no line break, with the notify placeholders.

max length 255; example "Lead {{ lead.first_name }} needs attention"
titlestring

create_task and notify (required): the title template, at most 255 characters and no line break. Placeholders are written as {{ name }}. A notify title may use lead.id, lead.first_name, lead.last_name, lead.email, lead.phone, lead.company, lead.status, lead.source (lead rules) or deal.id, deal.title, deal.stage, deal.amount, deal.currency, deal.source (deal rules), plus owner.first_name, owner.last_name, owner.email; a task title only lead.id, lead.status, lead.source (lead rules) or the deal placeholders (deal rules).

max length 255; example "Follow up with lead {{ lead.id }}"
user_idinteger

The staff user (active, not a customer) named by recipient user, and the new owner of an assign_owner step, where it is required. Refused (not_applicable) with recipient owner. An unknown, deactivated or customer user is refused with unknown_reference (400 INVALID_REFERENCE when it is the first refusal).

example 7
valueany

condition: the value compared against, as raw JSON whose type depends on the operator: a string for eq/neq/contains on a string field, a non-empty array of strings for in/not_in, a number for every operator on a number or money field (amount_cents in minor units), and no value (omitted or null) for is_empty/is_not_empty. The schema therefore names no type; an object is always refused.

models.AutomationTrigger

Trigger configuration of an automation rule. from and to apply to status or stage change triggers; hour_utc, filters and stale_days to the schedule trigger.

FieldTypeDescription
filtersmodels.AutomationScheduleFilter

Schedule trigger only: narrows the records each pass visits. Refused (not_applicable) on an event trigger.

fromstring

Only for lead_status_changed and deal_stage_changed: start only when the previous value was this lead status (lead rules) or deal stage (deal rules). Omitted or empty means any; on any other trigger type it is refused (not_applicable).

one of: "new", "contacted", "qualified", "unqualified", "converted", "qualification", "proposal", "negotiation", "won", "lost"; example "new"
hour_utcinteger

Schedule trigger only: the UTC hour (0-23) from which the daily pass may start; it starts at the first worker tick at or after that hour. Always present in responses (0 for event triggers); a non-zero value on an event trigger is refused (not_applicable).

minimum 0; maximum 23; example 8
stale_daysinteger

Schedule trigger only: visit only records whose updated_at is more than this many days old; 0 or omitted means any age. Must not be negative; refused (not_applicable) on an event trigger.

minimum 0; example 14
tostring

Only for lead_status_changed and deal_stage_changed: start only when the new value is this lead status (lead rules) or deal stage (deal rules). Omitted or empty means any; on any other trigger type it is refused (not_applicable).

one of: "new", "contacted", "qualified", "unqualified", "converted", "qualification", "proposal", "negotiation", "won", "lost"; example "qualified"

Origin allowlist: bare host names with an optional port, lower-cased, matched exactly (subdomains must be listed one by one). Empty allows every origin. A definition requested from another origin is a 404; a submission from another origin is stored as spam (reason domain). At most 20, each at most 255 characters. Reads return [] when there are none; the POST /forms and PUT /forms/{id} responses echo the request and carry null when the list was omitted or sent as null.

nullable; example ["www.example.com", "localhost:5173"]

models.Task

A to-do item assigned to one user, optionally linked to a lead or a customer (never both) and carrying labels.

FieldTypeDescription
assigned_toobject

The assignee's user record. Always present in the JSON. GET /tasks/{id}, PUT /tasks/{id}, GET /tasks/upcoming and the admin and label-filtered GET /tasks listings fill it in with the User object (see the User schema). POST /tasks, GET /tasks/my and the non-admin GET /tasks without label_id return a zero-valued placeholder instead (id 0, every string empty, role "" included, timestamps 0001-01-01T00:00:00Z), which is not a valid User, so this property is documented as a plain object. Use assigned_to_id.

assigned_to_idinteger

ID of the user the task is assigned to. Non-admins may only create tasks for themselves and only admins may change it.

FieldTypeDescription

Name of the decoy input the renderer must include and hide from people; always "website_url_confirm". A non-empty value in the submission files it as spam.

service.RuleFieldError

One refused field of an automation rule body.

FieldTypeDescription
codestring

Why it was refused: required (missing or blank), too_long, line_break (a title or subject with a line break), invalid_value (not an allowed value), out_of_range (a number outside its bounds, or waits adding up to more than 90 days), not_applicable (set where it does not apply), unknown_placeholder, too_many (more than 20 steps) or unknown_reference (an unknown, deactivated or customer user).

one of: "required", "too_long", "line_break", "invalid_value", "out_of_range", "not_applicable", "unknown_placeholder", "too_many", "unknown_reference"; example "required"
fieldstring

Path of the refused field in the request body: name, description, entity_type, trigger_type, trigger.from, trigger.to, trigger.hour_utc, trigger.stale_days, trigger.filters, trigger.filters.status, trigger.filters.stage, steps, steps[i].step_type or steps[i].config.<name>, with i the zero-based index in the steps array.

example "steps[0].config.title"
messagestring

Human-readable explanation; it may quote the refused value.

example "title is required"

utils.APIError

Error object of a failed request. code is the stable value to branch on; message is for people and may change.

FieldTypeDescription
codestring

Stable machine-readable error code. Shared codes: VALIDATION_ERROR, BAD_REQUEST, UNAUTHORIZED, FORBIDDEN, NOT_FOUND, CONFLICT, TOO_MANY_REQUESTS, INTERNAL_ERROR, MAIL_DELIVERY_FAILED; operations document their own additional codes (for example INVALID_REFERENCE, SESSION_STALE, SESSION_REFRESH_UNAVAILABLE).

example "VALIDATION_ERROR"
detailsany

Optional context; its JSON type depends on the code and the operation, so the schema leaves it open. Usually an object: a binding failure (VALIDATION_ERROR) maps each failed field to a message, keyed by the server-side Go field name (for example "Email", "FirstName"), not the JSON name; TOO_MANY_REQUESTS carries {"retry_after": "60s"}. Some operations send another shape (for example a plain string on POST /aeo/prompts/{id}/run with an invalid id) and document it; many errors omit it.

messagestring

Human-readable explanation. Not stable: do not parse it.

example "Validation failed"

example "3b1f8c2e-6d4a-4f0e-9a51-2c7d9e8b1a40"

utils.APIResponse

Envelope of every JSON response (the CSV export and the public form pages are the exceptions). success tells the outcome; data carries the payload on success, error the failure on error; meta is always present.

FieldTypeDescription
dataany

Operation-specific payload on success (each operation documents its shape). Absent on errors and on successes without a payload.

errorutils.APIError

Failure details; present only when success is false.

metautils.APIMeta

Request metadata, present on every enveloped response, with pagination fields on paginated lists.

successboolean

True for a 2xx response, false when error is set.

example true
